title: Keep entire checkpoints out of Vercel deployments
publishedAt: "2026-05-31"
---

When a GitHub repository is connected to Vercel, every push can become a Preview Deployment.

Most of the time, that is exactly what I want. A pull request gets a URL, checks show up next to CI, and the repository naturally becomes connected to a place where I can inspect the change.

But I recently ran into a small bit of noise.

In a project using `entire.io`, the branch `entire/checkpoints/v1` is pushed automatically. That branch exists for entire's checkpoints. It is useful state for the tool, but it is not an application branch that I want Vercel to deploy.

To Vercel, though, it is still a GitHub branch receiving pushes.

So every checkpoint push can produce another Preview Deployment. Nothing is broken, but the deployment list and GitHub checks start to include deployments that are not connected to the product surface I am trying to review.

The fix is small: tell Vercel that this branch should not be deployed.

### Configure the branch in vercel.json

Vercel supports branch-level deployment control through `git.deploymentEnabled` in `vercel.json`.

For `entire/checkpoints/v1`, the configuration looks like this:

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
  "git": {
    "deploymentEnabled": {
      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
    }
  }
}
```

If the project already has a `vercel.json`, keep the existing settings and add the `git` block next to them.

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
  "crons": [
    {
      "path": "/api/cron",
      "schedule": "*/5 * * * *"
    }
  ],
  "git": {
    "deploymentEnabled": {
      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
    }
  }
}
```

After this, pushes to `entire/checkpoints/v1` no longer trigger Vercel deployments. Other branches continue to deploy as usual.

### Prefer this over an ignored build command

Vercel also has Ignored Build Step / `ignoreCommand`, and that can be useful in other situations.

For this case, though, the intent is not "start a build and decide to ignore it." The intent is "this branch is not a deployment branch." Keeping that decision in `git.deploymentEnabled` makes the configuration easier to read later.

This is a tiny setting, but it feels like the kind of boundary that becomes more important as agent tools add their own state to a repository.

Checkpoints, metadata, state files, and tool-owned branches can all be useful. They help work resume and make automation more durable. But not all of that state needs to appear in the human review surface.

The checkpoint should remain a checkpoint. The Vercel deployment list should stay focused on branches that represent the application.

That small distinction keeps the tool useful without letting its internal bookkeeping become everyday deployment noise.

```

> AGENT

書きました。追加したのは以下です。

- [日本語版](/Users/toyamarinyon/.codex/worktrees/78f1/ultrahope.dev/apps/web/writing/entire-checkpoints-vercel/ja.md)
- [英語版](/Users/toyamarinyon/.codex/worktrees/78f1/ultrahope.dev/apps/web/writing/entire-checkpoints-vercel/en.md)

`entire/checkpoints/v1` が便利なcheckpoint用branchでありつつ、VercelのGit連携から見ると普通のpushなのでPreview Deploymentが増えてしまう、という流れで書いています。設定例は `git.deploymentEnabled` を中心にして、`ignoreCommand` よりこちらが意図に近い、という整理にしました。

確認は `bun run build` が通っています。`bun run format:check` は既存の `markdown-renderer.tsx` と `baseline/page.tsx` の整形差分で落ちましたが、今回追加したMarkdownは指摘対象ではありませんでした。

> DEVELOPER

とてもよく書けているし、toyamarinyonが書きそうな文体ですが、このブログのメッセージはとてもシンプルなのでそれに対しては色々お袈裟に言いすぎていると思います。もう少しシンプルにコンパクトにいきましょう

> AGENT

了解です。これはたしかに「小さな運用メモ」で十分に価値が出る話ですね。文体の温度は残しつつ、道具論のふくらみを削って、設定にすっと着地する短い記事に整えます。

  "workdir": "/Users/toyamarinyon/.codex/worktrees/78f1/ultrahope.dev",

title: Keep entire checkpoints out of Vercel deployments
publishedAt: "2026-05-31"
---

When a GitHub repository is connected to Vercel, every push can become a Preview Deployment.

Most of the time, that is exactly what I want. A pull request gets a URL, checks show up next to CI, and the repository naturally becomes connected to a place where I can inspect the change.

But I recently ran into a small bit of noise.

In a project using `entire.io`, the branch `entire/checkpoints/v1` is pushed automatically. That branch exists for entire's checkpoints. It is useful state for the tool, but it is not an application branch that I want Vercel to deploy.

To Vercel, though, it is still a GitHub branch receiving pushes.

So every checkpoint push can produce another Preview Deployment. Nothing is broken, but the deployment list and GitHub checks start to include deployments that are not connected to the product surface I am trying to review.

The fix is small: tell Vercel that this branch should not be deployed.

### Configure the branch in vercel.json

Vercel supports branch-level deployment control through `git.deploymentEnabled` in `vercel.json`.

For `entire/checkpoints/v1`, the configuration looks like this:

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
  "git": {
    "deploymentEnabled": {
      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
    }
  }
}
```

If the project already has a `vercel.json`, keep the existing settings and add the `git` block next to them.

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
  "crons": [
    {
      "path": "/api/cron",
      "schedule": "*/5 * * * *"
    }
  ],
  "git": {
    "deploymentEnabled": {
      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
    }
  }
}
```

After this, pushes to `entire/checkpoints/v1` no longer trigger Vercel deployments. Other branches continue to deploy as usual.

### Prefer this over an ignored build command

Vercel also has Ignored Build Step / `ignoreCommand`, and that can be useful in other situations.

For this case, though, the intent is not "start a build and decide to ignore it." The intent is "this branch is not a deployment branch." Keeping that decision in `git.deploymentEnabled` makes the configuration easier to read later.

This is a tiny setting, but it feels like the kind of boundary that becomes more important as agent tools add their own state to a repository.

Checkpoints, metadata, state files, and tool-owned branches can all be useful. They help work resume and make automation more durable. But not all of that state needs to appear in the human review surface.

The checkpoint should remain a checkpoint. The Vercel deployment list should stay focused on branches that represent the application.

That small distinction keeps the tool useful without letting its internal bookkeeping become everyday deployment noise.

title: entireのcheckpointをVercelに流さない
publishedAt: "2026-05-31"
---

VercelにGitHub repositoryを連携していると、pushのたびにPreview Deploymentが作られます。

普段は便利なのですが、`entire.io`を使っているprojectでは少しだけ注意が必要でした。

entireはcheckpointを保存するために、`entire/checkpoints/v1`というbranchを自動的にpushします。これはアプリケーションのPreviewを見たいbranchではないのですが、Vercelから見ると普通のGitHub branchへのpushです。

そのままだと、checkpointが更新されるたびにVercelのdeployが走ります。

壊れているわけではありません。ただ、deploy一覧やGitHub checksにアプリとは関係ないPreview Deploymentが増えていくので、少し面倒です。

### vercel.jsonで除外する

Vercelでは、`vercel.json`の`git.deploymentEnabled`でbranchごとの自動deployを無効化できます。

`entire/checkpoints/v1`を除外するなら、次のように書きます。

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
  "git": {
    "deploymentEnabled": {
      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
    }
  }
}
```

既存の`vercel.json`がある場合は、既存設定を残したまま`git`を追加します。

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
  "crons": [
    {
      "path": "/api/cron",
      "schedule": "*/5 * * * *"
    }
  ],
  "git": {
    "deploymentEnabled": {
      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
    }
  }
}
```

これで、`entire/checkpoints/v1`にpushされてもVercelの自動deployは走らなくなります。他のbranchはこれまで通りdeployされます。

### ignoreCommandではなくbranch設定にする

Vercelには`ignoreCommand`もありますが、今回やりたいことはbuildを途中で止めることではありません。

`entire/checkpoints/v1`は、そもそもdeploy対象ではないbranchです。

なので、`git.deploymentEnabled`で「このbranchはdeployしない」と書いておくほうが、あとから見ても意図がわかりやすいと思います。

小さい設定ですが、entireをVercel deploy対象のprojectで使うなら最初に入れておくと楽です。
---
title: Keep entire checkpoints out of Vercel deployments
publishedAt: "2026-05-31"
---

When a GitHub repository is connected to Vercel, every push can become a Preview Deployment.

That is usually convenient, but there is one small thing to watch for when using `entire.io`.

entire automatically pushes a branch named `entire/checkpoints/v1` to store checkpoints. That branch is useful for entire, but it is not an application branch that I want Vercel to deploy.

Without extra configuration, Vercel still sees it as a normal GitHub branch push.

So each checkpoint update can create another Preview Deployment. Nothing is broken, but the deployment list and GitHub checks get noisier.

### Exclude it in vercel.json

Vercel supports branch-level deployment control through `git.deploymentEnabled` in `vercel.json`.

For `entire/checkpoints/v1`, write:

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
  "git": {
    "deploymentEnabled": {
      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
    }
  }
}
```

If the project already has a `vercel.json`, keep the existing settings and add the `git` block.

```json
{
  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
  "crons": [
    {
      "path": "/api/cron",
      "schedule": "*/5 * * * *"
    }
  ],
  "git": {
    "deploymentEnabled": {
      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
    }
  }
}
```

After this, pushes to `entire/checkpoints/v1` no longer trigger Vercel deployments. Other branches continue to deploy as usual.

### Prefer branch config here

Vercel also has `ignoreCommand`, but this case is simpler than that.

The checkpoint branch is not a branch that should be deployed in the first place. Describing that in `git.deploymentEnabled` makes the intent clearer than stopping the build later.

It is a small setting, but if you use entire in a Vercel-deployed project, it is worth adding early.

---
title: Keep entire checkpoints out of Vercel deployments
publishedAt: "2026-05-31"
---

When a GitHub repository is connected to Vercel, every push can become a Preview Deployment.

That is usually convenient, but there is one small thing to watch for when using `entire.io`.

entire automatically pushes a branch named `entire/checkpoints/v1` to store checkpoints. That branch is useful for entire, but it is not an application branch that I want Vercel to deploy.

Without extra configuration, Vercel still sees it as a normal GitHub branch push.

diff --git a/apps/web/writing/exclude-entire-checkpoints-from-vercel/ja.md b/apps/web/writing/exclude-entire-checkpoints-from-vercel/ja.md
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..bc85df7
--- /dev/null
+++ b/apps/web/writing/exclude-entire-checkpoints-from-vercel/ja.md
@@ -0,0 +1,62 @@
+---
+title: entireのcheckpoint branchをVercelにdeployしない
+publishedAt: "2026-05-31"
+---
+
+VercelにGitHub repositoryを連携していると、pushのたびにPreview Deploymentが作られます。
+
+普段は便利なのですが、`entire.io`を使っているprojectでは少しだけ注意が必要でした。
+
+entireはcheckpointを保存するために、`entire/checkpoints/v1`というbranchを自動的にpushします。これはアプリケーションのPreviewを見たいbranchではないのですが、Vercelから見ると普通のGitHub branchへのpushです。
+
+そのままだと、checkpointが更新されるたびにVercelのdeployが走ります。
+
+壊れているわけではありません。ただ、deploy一覧やGitHub checksにアプリとは関係ないPreview Deploymentが増えていくので、少し面倒です。
+
+### vercel.jsonで除外する
+
+Vercelでは、`vercel.json`の`git.deploymentEnabled`でbranchごとの自動deployを無効化できます。
+
+`entire/checkpoints/v1`を除外するなら、次のように書きます。
+
+```json
+{
+  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
+  "git": {
+    "deploymentEnabled": {
+      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
+    }
+  }
+}
+```
+
+既存の`vercel.json`がある場合は、既存設定を残したまま`git`を追加します。
+
+```json
+{
+  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
+  "crons": [
+    {
+      "path": "/api/cron",
+      "schedule": "*/5 * * * *"
+    }
+  ],
+  "git": {
+    "deploymentEnabled": {
+      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
+    }
+  }
+}
+```
+
+これで、`entire/checkpoints/v1`にpushされてもVercelの自動deployは走らなくなります。他のbranchはこれまで通りdeployされます。
+
+### ignoreCommandではなくbranch設定にする
+
+Vercelには`ignoreCommand`もありますが、今回やりたいことはbuildを途中で止めることではありません。
+
+`entire/checkpoints/v1`は、そもそもdeploy対象ではないbranchです。
+
+なので、`git.deploymentEnabled`で「このbranchはdeployしない」と書いておくほうが、あとから見ても意図がわかりやすいと思います。
+
+小さい設定ですが、entireをVercel deploy対象のprojectで使うなら最初に入れておくと楽です。

```

diff --git a/apps/web/writing/exclude-entire-checkpoints-from-vercel/en.md b/apps/web/writing/exclude-entire-checkpoints-from-vercel/en.md
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..e2b99a6
--- /dev/null
+++ b/apps/web/writing/exclude-entire-checkpoints-from-vercel/en.md
@@ -0,0 +1,60 @@
+---
+title: Do not deploy entire checkpoint branches to Vercel
+publishedAt: "2026-05-31"
+---
+
+When a GitHub repository is connected to Vercel, every push can become a Preview Deployment.
+
+That is usually convenient, but there is one small thing to watch for when using `entire.io`.
+
+entire automatically pushes a branch named `entire/checkpoints/v1` to store checkpoints. That branch is useful for entire, but it is not an application branch that I want Vercel to deploy.
+
+Without extra configuration, Vercel still sees it as a normal GitHub branch push.
+
+So each checkpoint update can create another Preview Deployment. Nothing is broken, but the deployment list and GitHub checks get noisier.
+
+### Exclude it in vercel.json
+
+Vercel supports branch-level deployment control through `git.deploymentEnabled` in `vercel.json`.
+
+For `entire/checkpoints/v1`, write:
+
+```json
+{
+  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
+  "git": {
+    "deploymentEnabled": {
+      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
+    }
+  }
+}
+```
+
+If the project already has a `vercel.json`, keep the existing settings and add the `git` block.
+
+```json
+{
+  "$schema": "https://openapi.vercel.sh/vercel.json",
+  "crons": [
+    {
+      "path": "/api/cron",
+      "schedule": "*/5 * * * *"
+    }
+  ],
+  "git": {
+    "deploymentEnabled": {
+      "entire/checkpoints/v1": false
+    }
+  }
+}
+```
+
+After this, pushes to `entire/checkpoints/v1` no longer trigger Vercel deployments. Other branches continue to deploy as usual.
+
+### Prefer branch config here
+
+Vercel also has `ignoreCommand`, but this case is simpler than that.
+
+The checkpoint branch is not a branch that should be deployed in the first place. Describing that in `git.deploymentEnabled` makes the intent clearer than stopping the build later.
+
+It is a small setting, but if you use entire in a Vercel-deployed project, it is worth adding early.

```
